LinearRecurrence.IsSolutiondefMathlib.Algebra.LinearRecurrence, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $E$ be a linear recurrence of $R$. Let $u$ be a function from natural numbers to elements of $R$. Then $\operatorname{IsSolution}$ holds for $E$ and $u$, if for all natural numbers $n$, $u$ applied to the sum of $n$ and the order of $E$ is equal to $\FinsetSum {\FinsetunivMACRoAC}{i}{(\operatorname{coeffs} (E , i)\times u (n + \operatorname{val} (i)))}$.
Formula
$E \in \mathrm{LinearRecurrence} (R), u \in \Natural \rightarrow R \vdash \operatorname{IsSolution} (E , u):\Leftrightarrow \forall n \in \Natural . \EqtMACRoJ {u (n + \operatorname{order} (E))}{\FinsetSum {\FinsetunivMACRoAJ}{i}{(\operatorname{coeffs} (E , i)\times u (n + \operatorname{val} (i)))}}$
French
Soit $E$ un recurrence linéaire de $R$. Soit $u$ une fonction des nombres naturels à des éléments de $R$. Alors $\operatorname{IsSolution}$ tient pour $E$ et $u$ si pour tous les nombres naturels $n$, $u$ appliqué à la somme de $n$ et de l'ordre de $E$ est égal à $\FinsetSum {\FinsetunivMACRoAC}{i}{(\operatorname{coeffs} (E , i)\times u (n + \operatorname{val} (i)))}$.

Wording

French

Alors le polynôme caractéristique de $E$ est un polynôme de $R$ défini comme la différence entre $\operatorname{monomial}$ appliqué à l'ordre de $E$ et $1$ et $\FinsetSum {\FinsetunivMACRoAC}{i}{\operatorname{monomial} (\operatorname{val} (i), \operatorname{coeffs} (E , i))}$.

Wording
no wording
English
Let $p$ be an additive submonoid of $M$ such that for all elements $a$ of $A$, for all elements $m_{1}$ of $M$, if $m_{1}$ is an element of $p$, then $\HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {a}{m_{1}}$ is an element of $p$ and for all elements $b$ of $B$, for all elements $m$ of $M$, if $m$ is an element of $p$, then $\HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {b}{m}$ is an element of $p$. Then $\operatorname{mk}$ applied to $p$, $\mathrm{hA}$ and $\mathrm{hB}$ is a submodule of the set of tensor products of $A$ and $B$ and $M$.
Formula
$p \in \mathrm{AddSubmonoid} (M), \forall a \in A . \forall m_{1} \in M . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {p}{m_{1}}\rightarrow \MembershipmemMACRoAA {p}{\HSMulhSMulMACRoAT {a}{m_{1}}}, \forall b \in B . \forall m \in M . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {p}{m}\rightarrow \MembershipmemMACRoAA {p}{\HSMulhSMulMACRoAT {b}{m}}\vdash \operatorname{mk} (p , \mathrm{hA} , \mathrm{hB})\in \mathrm{Submodule} (\mathrm{TensorProduct} (R , A , B), M)$
French
Soit $p$ un sous-monoïde additif de $M$ tel que pour tous des éléments $a$ de $A$, pour tous des éléments $m_{1}$ de $M$, si $m_{1}$ est un élément de $p$, alors $\HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {a}{m_{1}}$ soit un élément de $p$ et pour tous des éléments $b$ de $B$, pour tous des éléments $m$ de $M$, si $m$ est un élément de $p$, alors $\HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {b}{m}$ soit un élément de $p$. Alors $\operatorname{mk}$ appliqué à $p$, $\mathrm{hA}$ et $\mathrm{hB}$ est un sous-module de l'ensemble de produits de tenseurs de $A$ et $B$ et $M$.
Subbimodule.toSubbimoduleIntdefMathlib.Algebra.Module.Bimodule, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $R$, $S$ and $M$ be sets. Let $p$ be a submodule of the set of tensor products of $R$ and $S$ and $M$. Then $\operatorname{toSubbimoduleInt}$ applied to $R$, $S$, $M$ and $p$ is a submodule of the set of tensor products of $R$ and $S$ and $M$ defined as the base change of $\Integer$ and $p$.
Formula

the linear equivalence of f and g (from the name)
English

Then the canonical isomorphism between the localization of $p$ and $\mathrm{M'}$ is a linear equivalence of the set of quotients of the set of localized modules of $p$ and $M$ and the localization of $p$ and $\mathrm{M'}$ and the set of localized modules of $p$ and the set of quotients of $M$ and $\mathrm{M'}$ defined as the scalar restriction of the set of localizations of $p$ and the linear equivalence of the localization map of $p$ and $\mathrm{M'}$ and the linear map of $p$ and the set of quotients of $M$ and $\mathrm{M'}$.
Formula
$p \in \mathrm{Submonoid} (R), \mathrm{M'} \in \mathrm{Submodule} (R , M)\vdash \mathrm{localizedQuotientEquiv} (p , \mathrm{M'}):= \operatorname{restrictScalars} (\mathrm{Localization} (p), \operatorname{linearEquiv} (p , \operatorname{toLocalizedQuotient} (p , \mathrm{M'}), \operatorname{mkLinearMap} (p , \operatorname{Quotient} (M , \mathrm{M'}))))\in \mathrm{LinearEquiv} (\operatorname{id} (\mathrm{Localization} (p)), \operatorname{Quotient} (\mathrm{LocalizedModule} (p , M), \operatorname{localized} (p , \mathrm{M'})), \mathrm{LocalizedModule} (p , \operatorname{Quotient} (M , \mathrm{M'})))$
French

Alors $\mathrm{SnakeLemma\_\_\_u3b4\_'}$ appliqué à $x_{19}$, $x_{20}$, $x_{21}$, $x_{22}$, $x_{23}$, $\mathrm{hf}$, $x_{24}$, $x_{25}$, $\mathrm{hg}$, $h$, $h_{1}$, $x_{32}$, $h_{2}$, $x_{33}$, $h_{3}$, $h_{4}$ et $h_{5}$ est une application linéaire de $x_{26}$ et $x_{27}$.
